## Document 022: 2026-10-14_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ_5c83f50b

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

日本の原油輸入価格は今後さらに上昇へ 
～物価上振れリスクが強まれば、一段の利上げ加速も～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
池田 亮平 
エコノミスト 阿部 大樹 
シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山 祐資 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 日銀は物価上振れ抑制に舵。中東情勢に起因する物価押し上げリスクは予断を許さず 
9 月日銀会合後の会見で植田総裁は「政策局面が変化した」と述べた（図表 1） 。これまで日銀は、
基調的物価の押し上げを金融政策運営の重点としてきたが、足元では 2％前後の物価上昇が定着しつ
つあるとみられる。こうしたもとで、金融政策運営の重点は物価上振れの回避に移っており、そのた
めに緩和的な金融環境をなるべく早く脱することが重視される。日銀の基本的なスタンスが、連続利
上げや、0.25％の利上げ幅を上回る大幅利上げは回避するのが望ましいというものであることも踏ま
えると、今後の利上げペースは 3 カ月に一回程度、0.25％ずつが基本になるだろう 1。 
ただし、金融政策の重点が物価上振れ回避にシフトしているなかでは、よりフォワードルッキング
な政策運営が求められる 2。したがって、 原油調達コストの上昇や円安など、 物価上振れリスクの強ま
り次第で利上げペースが速まる可能性がある。先行きの物価を押し上げる主なリスク要因として、日
図表1 9月会合後の総裁会見のポイント 図表2 WTI原油先物

### Page 2

2

銀は、原油高等の中東情勢影響、AI 関連需要の強さ、円安の三点を挙げている。このうち、原油価格
は足元で再び上昇しており、先行きの不確実性も大きい。中東情勢は、物価を取り巻く現下最大のリ
スクだろう。 
■ 不安定な情勢下で再び原油価格が上昇。タンカー運賃も急騰しダブルパンチの様相 
WTI原油先物は、9月に入って一時1バレル=100ドル台と、5月中旬以来の水準に上昇した （図表2） 。
足元では原油価格は幾分低下しているものの、水準としては依然高い状態が続いている。背景には、
中東産原油の供給懸念が強まったことがある。7月下旬以降、 サウジアラビアとイエメン・フーシ派に
よる戦闘が開始し、 ホルムズ海峡を代替する重要な原油輸送ルートとして活用されてきたバブ ・ エル ・
マンデブ海峡の通航リスクが高まった。 また、9月に入り、 サウジアラビアの原油パイプラインが攻撃
を受けて停止したと伝わると、中東産原油の域外輸送が一段と困難になるとの見方が広がった。 
中東情勢が再度悪化するなかで上昇しているのは原油価格だけではない。原油価格の上昇を大幅に
上回る勢いで、原油タンカーの運賃が急騰している（図表3）。8月以降に上昇基調が鮮明となり、9月
中旬には一時、中東情勢悪化前（2026年初）の約46倍、情勢悪化後の3～7月頃と比べても約3.2倍に達
した。 海峡航行リスクが高まるもと、 積み替えや迂回によって原油輸送の効率が悪化しているためだ。
海事データを活用して商品市場を分析しているベルギーの調査会社Kplerによると、 ホルムズ海峡の内
側にあるペルシャ湾から原油を運び出す船と、海峡の外側から最終目的地まで運ぶ船を分け、洋上で
原油を積み替える方式が広がっているようだ。 長距離輸送を担う船が危険な航路を通らずに済む反面、
積み替え作業や待機に時間を要するため、船を次の輸送に回しにくくなる。また、危険な海域を避け
るために遠回りする輸送も増え、船が一度の航海に費やす日数も長くなっている。より多くの輸送資
源が必要となったことで船舶需給がひっ迫し、タンカー運賃が大きく押し上げられている格好だ。

図表3 原油タンカーの運賃 図表4 原油輸入単価と原油価格

当たり換算収益。日本を含む東アジア向け運賃の代理指標。

の価格形成機能が損なわれたと指摘されているため。原油輸入単価

■ 今後は日本の原油調達コストが上昇。企業間取引価格は反転加速を予想 
原油価格の上昇に原油タンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの日本の原油輸入単価（単位量あたり
の原油調達コスト）は一段と上昇する見込みだ3。 中東産原油が日本に輸送されるまでには1～2カ月程
度の期間を要するため、8月中旬以降の原油価格上昇 ・原油タンカー運賃上昇は10月以降の原油輸入単
価を押し上げ、その後国内の企業間取引価格へと影響が波及していくと考えられる。足元の原油価格
とタンカー運賃を踏まえると、10月の原油輸入単価は1バレル＝約140ドル程度と、中東情勢悪化後で
最も高い水準に上昇する可能性が高い（図表4）。この影響を輸入全体の平均価格（円建てベース）で
みると、10月の伸びは前年比＋29％程度と、中東情勢悪化後に最も輸入物価の伸びが加速した6・7月
に迫る勢いになる計算だ。さらに、その後は、石油化学製品などの関連財を中心に、国内の企業間取
引価格が上昇しよう。 足元で上昇圧力が一服しつつある国内企業物価は再び加速し、11月頃に同＋7％
台の伸びになると予想する（図表5）。 
加えて、原油輸入金額が膨らんで貿易収支が悪化すれば、円安圧力が強まる可能性がある。原油の
代替調達が進展した6月以降は1兆円に迫る貿易赤字が続いているが、特に10月以降は原油輸入単価が
上昇するもとで本邦貿易赤字が拡大する公算が大きく、実需面からの円安圧力が強まりやすい（図表
6） 。 足元の為替相場は、 米国の利上げ観測を背景に円安に振れやすい地合いだ。 そこに実需の動きが
重なり、1ドル＝160円を上回るようなドル高円安が進行すれば、輸入物価の上振れを通じて、国内企
業物価の伸びが一層高まるリスクがある。 
■ 物価上昇圧力が長期化する場合は利上げペース加速も視野に 
以上のように、当面の企業間取引価格に対しては押し上げ圧力が持続する公算が大きい。物価上昇
圧力が長期化するほど消費者物価への価格転嫁姿勢も強まる可能性があるだけに、日銀は物価上振れ
に対する高い警戒レベルを維持することになろう。 
仮に物価上振れリスクが大きく高まる場合には、 連続利上げ ・大幅利上げの可能性も排除できない。
中東情勢の混乱が長期化し、27年以降も原油輸入単価が一段と上振れることが見込まれる場合、ある
図表5 輸入物価（円建て）と国内企業物価 図表6 貿易収支

### Page 4

4

いは1ドル=160円を持続的に上回る水準まで円安が進展する場合には 、連続利上げや大幅利上げのシ
ナリオが現実味を帯びてくるだろう。

1 中立金利の水準は不透明であり、利上げの影響はタイムラグをともなって現れる。連続利上げ・大幅利上げによって意図せず
金融引き締めに転じれば、経済へのマイナス影響が必要以上に強まるリスクがある。 
2 中東情勢悪化以降で最初の利上げを決定した 6 月会合を振り返っても、日銀は川上の企業物価の加速を理由の一つとして利上
げに踏み切っている。6 月会合の声明文において日銀は、国内企業物価上昇率が前年比＋6.3％（5 月時点の速報ベース）に加速
したことを踏まえ、 「企業間取引におけるやや速いスピードでの価格転嫁が、消費者段階における幅広い品目の価格上昇に波及
していく可能性がある」と評価した。 
3 中東情勢悪化以降、日本の原油輸入単価は国際市況から大きく上振れている。背景として、主な代替調達先である米国産原油
が需給ひっ迫の懸念等から値上がりし、割高になったことなどが挙げられる。経済産業省発表によると、秋口以降は中東産原油
の調達が増加し、割高な米国産原油に対する依存度は低下すると見込まれるものの、本文記載のように中東産原油の輸送コスト
が大幅に高まっていることから、先行きも割高感の解消は見込みづらい。

科技動能支撐股市，利率風險牽動債市配置
2026 年 9 月
全球股市 8 月截至 26 日多數上漲，其中成熟市場上漲 3.1%居冠，新興亞洲、東協股市與標普
500 也分別上漲 2.9%、2.7%及 2.6%。美股受企業財報韌性與月初就業、通膨數據降溫支撐；亞洲
則受美元偏弱、油價與長端殖利率回落帶動風險偏好改善。然而，中國股市期間下跌 0.3%，該國 7
月零售、工業生產與投資數據偏弱，加上阿里巴巴折價配股一度衝擊科技股，惟公司高層後續增持有
助緩解信心壓力 。 拉美股市下跌0.8%，在主要股市中敬陪末座 ， 主要拖累來自巴西 ；8 月截至 24 日，

外資淨流出巴西股市約 214 億雷亞爾，加上財政與 10 月大選的不確定性壓抑市場。印度亦小跌
0.3%，因高油價與 RBI 偏鷹訊號使公債殖利率上升。
全球債市 8 月截至 26 日則全面收紅 ， 以新興當地債上漲1.53%居冠 ， 美元指數回落至99 附近，
為其帶來匯兌加成；亞洲高收益債與全球高收益債分別上漲 1.16%、1.09%，高票息與較短存續期提
供支撐 。 亞洲債券 、 全球主權債與新興美元債表現相對落後 。8 月中美國 30 年期殖利率一度升至 2007
年以來高點，壓抑長存續期債券；其後油價回落，加上8/19 財政部宣布 9/9 起將 10 至 30 年期公債
買回上限至少倍增至每次 40 億美元，長端流動性疑慮獲緩解，30 年期殖利率回落，因而帶動債券價
格修復。
展望 9 月，市場焦點將集中於 9 月 15 日至 16 日的 FOMC 會議，以及最新經濟預測與點陣圖；
全球股市預料呈現高檔震盪格局。儘管物價漲勢稍有緩和，但 Fed 對通膨仍保持警戒；若政策立場
較市場預期鷹派，可能推升美債殖利率波動，並壓抑高估值成長股走勢。不過，AI 基礎建設與企業

科技投資趨勢延續，美國企業獲利成長亦有利科技股表現，預料中期基本面仍具支撐。台灣受 AI 與
半導體出口需求帶動，經濟成長動能強勁；日股則持續受企業治理改革與資本效率改善支撐。整體而
言，9 月股市短線震盪難免，但中期仍看好科技股，以及基本面相對強勁的台灣與日本股市。
就債市而言，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔；若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利
率上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短的存續期間亦伴隨再投資
風險；10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。相較之下，3 至 10 年期債券兼具較高息收、
適度存續期間與參與利率下行的空間。整體而言，目前 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬
條件相對均衡，較具表現機會。

美國股市：AI 熱潮推升企業獲利，美股續強但留意利率風險
八月美股在高檔震盪中收高，科技股表現亮眼，除整體美股優異財報支撐指數表現外，AI 龍頭企
業如 NVIDIA 均給出強勁展望 ，AI 行情由晶片硬體向軟體與資安應用端擴散 。指數上揚主要由輝達、
微軟、亞馬遜、帕蘭提爾、特斯拉、Meta、美光科技、賽富時、Marvell、默克藥廠等企業帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括蘋果、博通、沃爾瑪、TJX、AppLovin、威騰、聯合健康、CVS、應用材料、
超微半導體等。
聯準會新任主席試圖以新的溝通模式取代傳統前瞻指引，市場解讀為基準利率有上行空間且更長

時間維持高檔。聯準會主席華許在全球央行年會(Jackson Hole)拒絕提供傳統的前瞻指引或機械式的
反應函數，強調央行過度承諾未來利率路徑會失去政策彈性，以一套「決策準則」取代指引，重點觀
察潛在通膨是否快速回到 2%、勞動市場是否偏離充分就業，以及金融條件是否有效限制總需求。由
於 7 月 FOMC 會議出現官員主張升息的情況，使短端與長端美債殖利率走勢分化，殖利率曲線進一
步陡峭化 ， 市場正在質疑 Fed 是否具備一致且可理解的決策框架 ， 但在華許發言後 ， 按照這套標準，
目前政策訊號仍偏向利率在較高水準維持更長時間。由於通膨問題仍具廣度，同時信用利差偏低、股
市高檔、AI 投資與私人需求持續強勁，很難將目前金融條件視為具有限制性，故市場預期 聯準會可
能不只提高升息機率，也提高了升息後維持高利率的可能性，甚至進一步提高未來降息的門檻。
第二季財報公布大致落幕，表現優於先前預期。目前標普 500 成分股已有 459 家公布 2Q26 財
報，其中 86%獲利優於市場預期，並帶動 2Q26 獲利年增達約 31.6%，較季初市場預估的 21%大幅

上修；全年獲利年增亦由 23%上修至 27%。類股獲利年增動能以能源 (YoY+145%)、資訊科技
(YoY+71%)、原材料(YoY+41%)增長動能最強，醫療保健類股(YoY-7%)表現落後。資訊科技類股獲
利成長主要由半導體族群帶動，次產業中半導體獲利年增 139%、科技硬體與設備獲利年增 50.9%。
美國期中選舉前的「資料中心用電排擠效應引發的民粹反彈」持續發酵。隨著 AI 資料中心龐大
的電力需求，引發地方民意對民生與傳統產業用電資源遭擠壓的疑慮持續升溫 。 各州政府在選舉壓力
下，部分地方政府緊急凍結資料中心計畫，預料 11 月期中選舉後政治干擾將可降溫。
目前標普 500 指數位於歷史高檔位置，本益比已升至 23.51 倍、股價淨值比達 5.6 倍，短期評價
偏高。雖然美國科技業者目前仍居 AI 產業領先地位，但股市漲幅已高，短期仍需留意市場過熱與估
值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：景氣溫和復甦，歐洲央行升息預期與政治變數限制評價 
八月歐股延續多頭走勢，泛歐600 指數中原物料與科技股表現相對強勢，包括思愛普、艾司摩爾
控股、Argenx、施耐德電機、力拓、嘉能可、英美資源、安聯集團、勞斯萊斯控股、西門子等表現
較佳；相較之下，部分金融與消費股表現偏弱，包括雀巢、匯豐控股、諾和諾德、英美菸草、阿斯特
捷利康、法國巴黎銀行、法國興業銀行、諾華、百威英博、萬喜等。 
歐元區八月綜合 PMI 升至 52.1，連續第 2 個月站上榮枯線，顯示第 3 季初景氣延續擴張。其中
製造業升至 52.8，創 2022 年 5 月以來最佳表現，復甦已由生產端擴散至需求端。AI 相關科技與設
備需求增加，加上國防支出擴大，帶動製造業需求回升；服務業則持平於 51.7，受惠於旅遊維持溫
和擴張。國家別來看，德國製造業 PMI 升至 54.1，創 2022 年以來最佳表現，法國製造業重返擴張
區間，但極端高溫拖累服務業活動。整體而言，八月 PMI 顯示歐元區景氣動能明顯改善，製造業加
速復甦，短期衰退風險進一步下降，然而服務業擴張力道未見加快，復甦步調呈現分化。 
部分歐洲央行官員表示，九月已有充分理由升息，利率交換市場目前反映 ECB 在 9 月會議升息
一碼的可能性。美伊戰爭推高能源成本，加上歐洲經濟表現穩健，使市場認為 ECB 仍有收緊貨幣政
策的空間。市場焦點因此由「ECB 會不會升息」轉向「升息循環還會延續多久」 。中東局勢仍是最大
變數，若能源成本持續走高且逐步傳導至商品、服務價格與薪資，ECB 第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場。 
9 月歐股須留意政治局勢。若 AfD（德國另類選擇黨）於地方選舉取得大勝，雖不會直接改變目
前政府執政地位，但可能削弱改革推進速度，造成市場風險溢價上升；另外英國新任首相柏南承諾維
持既有財政規則，並受到工黨不提高所得稅、國民保險及主要稅率承諾的約束，使政府在國防、公共
服務與生活成本支出增加之際 ， 財政操作空間相當有限 ， 市場因此高度關注政府是否轉向其他形式的
增稅。若改革措施過度激進推高英國公債殖利率，恐將造成市場震盪。 
歐洲經濟整體呈現溫和復甦，但原油價格再次走高是最大隱憂。若能源成本居高不下，歐洲央行
與英國央行第四季仍可能維持鷹派立場 ， 進而導致產業表現分化 。 銀行與金融類股可望受惠於利率走
高及淨利差改善；反之，科技、醫療保健及其他成長型類股將面臨殖利率上升所帶來的評價壓力。目
前泛歐 600 指數本益比約 16.5 倍，股價淨值比約 2.5 倍。雖然俄烏戰爭與通膨再度升溫風險仍需關
注，但在評價合理的背景下，歐股仍適合作為長線分批布局標的。

資料來源：彭博,2026/8/31

日本股市：通膨回溫拉高升息預期，半導體與內需動能推升經濟表現
八月日股受地緣政治風險與殖利率走高影響，日經225 指數劇烈震盪。盤面上，強勢族群包括瑞
可利控股、愛德萬測試、藤倉、軟銀、松下控股、村田製作所、瑞穗金融、東京威力科創、三菱UFJ
金融、揖斐電等；弱勢股包括東京海上控股、迅銷、大金工業、富士軟片、日本電裝、發那科、7&I、
丸紅、損保控股、KDDI 等。
8 月東京核心 CPI 升至 1.8%，雖能源補貼與食品價格降溫壓抑整體物價，但租金與醫療服務價
格明顯上升；扣除食品與能源後通膨加速至 1.4%，顯示內生性通膨壓力仍在。若就十大產品分項來

看，引導本月通膨的上漲主要來自房屋及醫療保健，其中租金價格年增幅達 1.9%，反映物價上漲持
續傳遞至房市 ； 醫療保健價格年增幅亦自前月的1.4%攀升至 2.8%， 因政府將高額療養費制度內的自
付費用上限調高所致 。 根據總務省估算 ， 日本政府對能源補貼的政策約壓抑本月通膨0.27 個百分點，
顯示若扣除政策影響，則當前通膨約為 2.2%，仍高於日本央行所設定的物價目標。雖政府補貼政策
將持續對通膨帶來壓抑效果，使得整體通膨略低於 2%，然而搭配央行對日圓匯率過於弱勢、恐進一
步墊高通膨的擔憂，預估將促使日本央行最快於 9 月份升息一碼。
日本半導體製造裝置協會 （SEAJ） 公布7 月日本製晶片設備銷售額 （3 個月移動平均值） 為5,562.2
億日圓，月銷售額年增 35.4%，創下 2024 年 12 月以來最大增幅。同時，若觀察今年 1 至 7 月份累
計銷售額達 3.44 兆日圓，年增 15.7%，顯示隨著當前人工智慧墊高晶片需求，推升廠商對半導體設
備的需求。
日本 7 月外國旅客入境人數為 344.2 萬人次，年增 0.1%。本月份外國旅客人數最大宗仍是來自

南韓，達 89.5 萬人，年增 31.9%。其次則為台灣遊客的 76.4 萬人次，年增 26.4%。與之相反，來
自中國的遊客人數年減幅達 56.1%， 因日中兩國關係遲遲未見改善所致 。 根據日本百貨店協會公布的
資料顯示，7 月百貨公司免稅銷售額年增 25.5%至 506 億日圓，連續五個月的正成長，顯示在日圓
延續弱勢的格局之下，外國旅客消費力仍有提升空間。
2026 年來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、日圓貶值、高市擴大財政刺激與強化軍事安全等政
策利多支撐，雖然日圓在政府干預與 BOJ 升息下繼續貶值空間受限，出口企業的匯兌紅利將逐步消
退，但預料日圓接下來仍以區間整理機會較高，並無大幅升值空間。目前 TOPIX 指數本益比約 17.7
倍、股價淨值比約 1.9 倍，評價仍屬合理，可利用市場震盪機會逢低分批布局。

台灣股市：工業生產創高、景氣續亮紅燈，AI 供應鏈撐起製造業擴張動能
8 月台股在 AI 龍頭財報優於預期及全球 AI 資本支出持續擴張帶動下，市場氣氛維持偏多，但受
到 Jackson Hole 會議後市場重新調整聯準會政策預期、美債殖利率攀升及美元轉強影響，指數於高
檔呈現震盪整理。資金面上，高評價 AI 族群漲多後出現輪動，市場開始重新檢視企業未來獲利成長
能否持續支撐評價水準。不過整體而言，AI 資料中心、半導體、先進封裝及伺服器供應鏈需求仍維
持強勁，有助於消化部分評價壓力，並使市場焦點重新回到企業基本面與獲利成長。

當前臺灣經濟仍處於景氣擴張階段。全球 AI 建置需求推動企業設備投資與科技資本支出維持高
檔，相關需求正逐步由晶片擴散至資料中心、網通設備、電力基礎建設及相關零組件。從國際環境觀
察，美國企業投資及私人需求維持韌性，AI 相關支出已成為支撐美國與全球科技景氣的重要力量，
也持續為台灣出口與半導體供應鏈提供結構性成長動能。
展望 9 月，市場關注焦點將由企業財報逐漸轉向總體經濟數據與聯準會政策方向。Jackson Hole
會議後，市場對於聯準會維持較高利率期間的預期升溫，短期可能提高全球金融市場波動。然而，從
企業基本面觀察，NVIDIA 最新展望顯示 AI 需求仍具高度能見度，全球雲端業者與大型企業的 AI 基
礎建設支出並未出現明顯放緩跡象。在此背景下，只要半導體、伺服器及先進封裝產業維持強勁接單
動能，台灣電子產業景氣仍有機會延續高檔表現。
投資策略方面，仍可維持對 AI 供應鏈的正向看法，但須更重視獲利成長與評價合理性。除了先
進製程及先進封裝外，AI 伺服器組裝、散熱、電源管理、高速傳輸、網通設備及記憶體等受惠 AI 基

建擴張之族群，仍具備中長期成長機會。隨著AI 投資逐步由 GPU 擴散至記憶體、網路交換器、光通
訊及系統整合等領域 ， 受惠範圍亦由少數龍頭供應商進一步擴展至整體供應鏈 ， 有助於支撐相關企業
未來營收與獲利成長動能。另一方面，9 月需留意美國 CPI 數據、聯準會利率決策及美債殖利率變化
對高評價科技股的影響；若市場因政策預期、資金輪動或短期獲利了結而出現回檔，較可能屬於多頭
趨勢中的震盪整理，而非基本面反轉。在企業獲利持續成長、AI 資本支出維持高檔及全球科技景氣
延續復甦的背景下 ， 建議布局方向可由單純題材面轉向具備技術門檻 、 訂單能見度及現金流品質較佳
的個股，以提升投資組合在景氣循環與市場波動中的穩健度。

中國股市：硬科技 IPO 熱潮延燒，A 股中報揭露「增收不增利」普遍現象
8 月中國經濟延續「總量承壓、科技產業相對強勢」的分化格局。儘管房地產調整與內需復甦力
道仍有限，但高端製造、人工智慧及數位經濟仍是政策資源與市場資金聚焦方向。在傳統產業景氣復
甦較為緩慢的情況下 ， 科技產業逐漸成為支撐經濟成長的重要動能 ， 顯示中國經濟正持續朝新舊動能
轉換與產業升級方向發展。
科技產業方面，人形機器人已成為近期最受市場關注的新興產業之一。根據中國國家市場監管總
局統計，2026 年上半年人形機器人領域新設企業數量達 11.6 萬家，較 2025 年同期的 7.2 萬家大幅

成長 61.1%，反映資本投入、技術研發與產業參與程度快速提升。隨著人工智慧、大模型及感測技術
進步，人形機器人正逐步由研發驗證邁向商業 化應用階段，產業發展重點也由概念題材轉向量產能
力、產品可靠度及終端需求驗證。
資本市場表現雖顯示科技板塊仍是 A 股資金主要聚焦方向 ， 但企業基本面改善幅度與市場期待之
間仍存在落差。從滬深 300 成分股來看，科技類企業近年營收維持增長，但獲利表現並未同步大幅
提升，反映部分企業仍面臨研發投入增加、產能擴張及市場競爭加劇等壓力。AI、半導體及高端製造
雖持續獲得政策支持與資本關注 ， 但不少企業的估值已提前反映未來成長預期 ， 實際獲利貢獻仍有待
進一步驗證。市場關注焦點也逐漸由政策題材與自主替代概念，轉向訂單能見度、現金流與獲利能力
等基本面指標；若後續營運表現未能跟上市場預期，不排除科技板塊仍面臨評價修正壓力。
展望 9 月，政策方向預計仍以穩增長與培育新質生產力為主軸，人工智慧、人形機器人、高端製
造及半導體等領域仍將是支持重點 。 在整體經濟復甦力道尚未全面擴散的背景下 ， 市場表現預期仍將

呈現結構性分化，資金持續聚焦具成長潛力與技術門檻的科技產業。投資策略上，可留意人形機器人
產業鏈、工業自動化、AI 應用、半導體設備及高端製造等受惠產業升級趨勢之族群；惟部分熱門題
材評價已反映較高成長預期，後續仍須觀察訂單落地、量產進度及獲利改善情況，避免因短期市場情
緒過熱而提高投資風險。

科技類股：AI 基礎建設持續擴張，算力、光通訊與平台生態同步受惠
全球科技產業的核心成長動能仍來自 AI 基礎建設擴張 ， 但市場焦點已逐步由單純GPU 採購轉向
整體資料中心架構升級 。 隨Google 擴大投入光通訊與高速網路技術 ，AI 資料中心正朝向更高頻寬、
更低延遲及更高能效方向發展 。 市場預估800G 至 1.6T 光通訊滲透率將持續提升 ， 推動光模組 、CPO、
交換器及相關供應鏈需求成長，也反映大型雲端服務供應商仍持續強化 AI 基礎建設布局。在此趨勢
下，AI 投資已由單一晶片擴展至網路、儲存、記憶體及先進封裝等完整生態系，帶動相關供應鏈重

新評價。
企業獲利方面，NVIDIA 最新財報再次驗證 AI 投資循環仍具延續性。公司第二季營收與資料中心
業務均大幅優於預期，資料中心網路業務亦維持高速成長，顯示大型企業、雲端服務業者及 AI 模型
開發商對算力需求仍然強勁。同時，管理層對第三季營收及新一代 Vera Rubin 平台維持正向展望，
反映 AI 基礎建設建置並未出現明顯放緩跡象。不過，市場對AI 產業的評價標準已逐漸由需求成長轉
向獲利品質與投資效益，未來資本支出、折舊攤提與現金流變化仍將是投資人關注重點。
從產業鏈角度來看，AI 需求正持續向外擴散。除了 GPU 之外，高速光通訊、網路交換器、高頻
寬記憶體(HBM)、CPU 及資料中心電力架構均成為下一階段重要受惠領域。另一方面，NVIDIA 近期
宣布結合金融機構與雲端服務資源打造大型 AI 基礎設施平台，顯示產業發展正由硬體銷售進一步延
伸至算力服務與平台營運模式。此舉有利強化 AI 產業生態系並提高資本取得效率，但也反映未來競
爭重點將由單純硬體性能逐步轉向平台整合能力、商業模式及資金運用效率。隨著更多企業投入 AI

建設，市場也開始關注高額資本支出是否能持續帶來相應回報，產業獲利表現仍可能出現明顯分化。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但市場關注焦點正由 「建置多少算力」逐步
轉向「創造多少效益」。NVIDIA 新平台推廣進度、Vera Rubin 產品放量、高速光通訊滲透率提升
及 HBM 供給變化，將是觀察下一階段 AI 投資循環的重要指標。雖然大型雲端業者資本支出尚未出
現明顯下修，支撐整體供應鏈需求維持高檔，但隨著資料中心建置成本、能源支出與折舊費用持續增
加 ， 企業獲利能力與現金流表現的重要性亦將提升 。 未來較具競爭優勢的企業 ， 除了掌握核心技術外 ，
更需具備規模化商業模式及資本效率，才能在 AI 產業由高速成長邁向成熟發展的過程中持續創造價
值。

礦業類股：關稅預期有利銅價支撐，戰略庫存需求是潛在利多
8 月以來礦業類股走勢強勁，倫敦金屬交易所銅價也再創歷史新高，這波上漲的主要動能，仍是
來自於我們先前一再提到的美國潛在銅關稅，改變了全球銅庫存的流向 。 由於市場擔心美國未來可能
對精煉銅加徵關稅，交易商提前將銅運往美國，希望在關稅實施前完成進口，導致其他地區能夠取得
的現貨供應相應減少，在供給減少下持續為銅價帶來支撐。
截至 8 月底，美國現行關稅主要涵蓋銅棒、銅線、銅板及銅管等半成品與衍生產品，精煉銅尚未
正式納入課稅範圍，但市場普遍預估美國政府將有可能在 2027 年開始實施精煉銅關稅，帶動交易商

提前採取備貨行動。路透社報導指出，美國2026 年上半年進口約 88.5 萬噸精煉銅，年增約 3%，紐
約商品交易所銅庫存也攀升至約 67.5 萬噸的歷史高點。若將時間拉長至一年觀察，全球三大交易所
合計增加約 49.58 萬噸庫存，其中約 81%集中於紐約商品交易所，顯示全球可見銅庫存雖然上升，
但新增庫存大多被鎖在美國，非美市場銅庫存相對處於較為緊張的狀態。
除了關稅帶動的庫存 失衡外，銅的實際應用需求也具有基本面支撐。彭博行業研究 預估，2026
年全球銅需求將成長 2.4%至 2.7%，增速雖較 2025 年放緩，但電動車、再生能源、資料中心及電網
升級仍將帶動用銅需求上升。其中雖然中國銅需求成長預估也將放緩，但是中國國家電網已規劃 2026
至 2030 年投資 4 兆元人民幣，較前五年增加 44%，以支應人工智慧用電需求。
另一方面，隨著區域銅庫存出現短缺及 AI 帶動用銅需求上升，若政府與企業在未來計畫提高安
全庫存，將可能成為市場尚未充分反映的中長期上漲基礎。花旗研究估算，目前全球精煉銅總庫存約

為 300 萬噸，僅相當於 1.3 個月的全球消費量。若各國政府及大型供應鏈基於國防、能源安全、人
工智慧基礎建設或地緣政治風險 ， 將安全庫存提高至兩個月 ， 市場約需要額外累積 170 萬噸銅庫存；
若提高至三個月，額外需求更將擴大至約 400 萬噸。由於新礦山從開發到投產需要多年，供給無法
在短期內迅速增加，市場只能透過銅價上漲，促使更多廢銅進入市場，同時迫使部分使用者節約用銅
或尋找替代材料。因此，建立庫存的規模愈大、完成時間愈短，銅價需要調整的漲幅也將愈顯著。
展望後市，儘管美國精煉銅關稅目前尚未正式實施，但市場預期帶來的提前備貨潮仍有利短線銅
價支撐，同時全球用銅需求仍維持成長，新礦山供給又難以快速實現，加上政府及企業可能逐步提高
安全庫存 ， 使銅市場的中長期供需結構仍具有支撐 ， 故對具銅價曝險的礦業類股中長期後市仍可正向
看待 ， 然而仍需留意若未來銅關稅未如市場預期實施 ， 當前囤積於美國市場的銅庫存將可能回歸到全
球市場當中，為銅價帶來短線波動。

能源類股：荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，惟實際供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期
8 月以來，美伊衝突持續反覆升降溫，雙方時而釋出談判訊號，時而再度發生軍事衝突，短期內
仍未出現實質進展，荷莫茲海峽通行亦持續受限。然而，海峽受阻並不代表原有運量全數消失，部分
原油仍可經由海運與陸上替代管線出口 ，同時中國石油需求下降及各國政府釋放戰略庫存亦抵銷部分
中斷影響。
彭博行業研究指出，儘管當前荷莫茲海峽受到干擾，但實際並未呈現完全封鎖的狀態，目前仍有
零星油輪持續通行。彭博指出，由於部分油輪會關閉公開定位訊號，並在通過海峽後於阿曼外海進行

轉運，因此公開船舶資料可能會漏算部分運量。彭博綜合目前庫存變化與市場實際供需後，估計目前
每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油通過荷莫茲海峽，意味僅依據可觀察船舶數量推算中東石油供應中斷量，
可能高估了市場實際缺口。
除了每日仍有約 750 萬桶原油經過海峽運輸外，彭博估計目前另有每日約 500 萬桶石油改走陸
上管線；需求端則因中國每日減少約 400 萬桶進口而下降，其他地區減少用油與各國政府釋放戰略
庫存再合計填補每日約 200 萬桶石油缺口。經過上述調整後，原本每日約 2,000 萬桶經荷莫茲海峽
通行的運量中，實際供應缺口僅剩每日約 150 萬桶，這也解釋了當前油價漲幅為何未完全反映表面
的產量損失。
全球石油庫存變化的數據亦呼應了實際缺口低於市場普遍預期的論點。 花旗報告指出 ，2026 年 2
月至 8 月全球實際觀察到的石油供需缺口平均約為每日 290 萬桶，明顯低於其原先預估的每日 700
萬至 800 萬桶。截至 2026 年 8 月，全球石油庫存仍可覆蓋 99 天需求，亦仍處於 2022 年至 2025

年的 95 至 101 天區間。花旗估計，若目前庫存下降速度延續，全球庫存要到 2029 年第一季才會降
至接近第二次石油危機緊張程度的 70 天水位，這顯示荷莫茲海峽若持續受阻，雖油價上漲壓力仍會
逐步累積，但實際庫存下降速度並未如市場普遍預期的嚴峻。
展望後市， 除荷莫茲海峽中斷造成的 供應缺口與庫存降幅低於市場預期 外，國際能源署 也預估
2027 年全球石油需求將增加每日 240 萬桶 ， 供應則可能年增每日830 萬桶 ， 意味若中東產量與海峽
通行逐步恢復，供應回升速度將明顯快於需求，並帶動庫存回補，不利油價上漲趨勢，然而考量短線
地緣風險仍有利能源類股形成支撐，在美伊達成長期停火協議前，應中性看待能源類股後市表現。

黃金類股：央行買盤支撐中長期價格，惟升息預期帶動短線波動升高
8 月金價走勢先揚後抑。月初公布的美國就業數據偏弱，加上中國及全球黃金 ETF 買盤回升，使
市場一度降低近期升息預期，金價因而突破 7 月的整理區間。到了 8 月中旬，美國財政部宣布擴大
長天期公債回購，帶動長端殖利率與美元回落，進一步放大黃金漲勢；不過，月底公布的通膨數據仍
顯示物價壓力具有黏性 ， 聯準會主席華許在傑克森霍爾會議的態度也偏向鷹派 ， 使市場重新提高升息
預期，金價隨即自高檔回落。然而即使月底出現修正，金價在 8 月仍累積明顯漲幅。
支撐金價中長期走勢的核心力量，仍然來自世界各國央行為分散外匯儲備，持續增持黃金。世界

黃金協會指出，2026 年第 2 季全球央行淨購金量為 289 噸，累計 2026 年上半年約 345 噸。雖然上
半年央行購金量受到土耳其、俄羅斯等國調節部位影響，降至2022 年以來同期低點，但第 2 季購金
量已較第 1 季明顯回升，而且買盤仍分布於波蘭、中國、烏茲別克及哈薩克等多個市場，顯示各國央
行增持黃金的趨勢仍未出現轉變，且可觀察到各國央行對於逢低承接黃金的意願。
在央行需求之外，中國投資資金是本輪金價突破的重要邊際買盤。2026 年上半年中國黃金淨進
口量達 764 噸，較 2025 年同期增加 138%，其規模明顯高於中國人民銀行公布的增持量，顯示民間
與機構投資人也在提高黃金配置 。 當中國資金推動金價突破整理區間後 ， 美國商品期貨交易委員會的
資料也顯示 ， 管理基金黃金淨多單在 8 月持續增加 ， 使原本集中於亞洲的漲勢進一步獲得美國期貨資
金支持。
然而，投資資金參與程度提高，也使金價對利率與美元變化更加敏感。美國財政部 8 月 19 日宣
布，將至少加倍 10 年至 30 年期公債的流動性支持回購規模，市場隨即下調長端殖利率，黃金也在

持有成本下降後快速上漲。不過，月底公布的美國 7 月通膨數據仍高，加上華許在傑克森霍爾會議維
持偏鷹立場，再次使市場重新認為聯準會仍可能透過升息抑制通膨，美元與殖利率回升後，部分短線
資金便出現獲利了結賣壓。
展望後市，各國央行增持黃金儲備仍支撐黃金的中長期多頭結構，而美國財政與債務可持續性疑
慮也將持續提高黃金的分散風險價值。不過，8 月底的回檔顯示，當金價漲勢由 ETF 及期貨資金放大
後，實質利率與美元只要轉為上升，短線市場部位便可能迅速調整，故在美國後續利率政策仍具有不
確定性的狀況下，對於黃金類股後市應中性看待。

固定收益：長端風險仍存，3~10 年期公債、投資級債更具表現機會
8 月以來，美國公債殖利率維持高檔震盪，若以 7 月底與 8/24 比較，各年期債券殖利率近乎平
行小幅下行。8 月初公布的 7 月非農就業意外減少 2.3 萬人，顯示勞動市場動能轉弱；7 月 CPI 年增
率由 3.5%降至 3.4%，核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.6%降至 2.5%，一度緩解市場對 Fed 緊縮疑慮。惟 7
月 PPI 與 PCE 數據表現，限制利率進一步下行空間。
8 月中旬之後，市場焦點由政策利率逐步轉向能源價格、財政供給與期限溢酬。美伊局勢反覆推

升油價，加上美國聯邦債務8 月首度突破 40 兆美元，2 年與 30 年期利差由 7 月底 99 個基點擴大至
8/18 的 109 個基點，30 年期殖利率亦升至 5.28%。美國財政部 8/19 宣布，自 9/9 起將部分 10～
30 年期公債單次流動性回購上限由 20 億美元提高至至少 40 億美元，帶動 30 年期殖利率由 5.28%
快速回落至 5.19%。截至 8/24，2 年與 30 年利差亦回到 99 個基點，月中陡峭化大致回吐。此一走
勢顯示 ，回購措施雖有助改善長券流動性 ， 但財政赤字 、公債供給及期限溢酬等結構性壓力仍未消失 。
全球主要成熟國家的長端債券亦面臨類似壓力。德國 30 年期公債殖利率近期升至約 3.79%，創
2011 年以來最高；日本 10 年期公債殖利率 8/18 盤中一度觸及 2.945%，亦為 1996 年 9 月以來高
位，30 年期亦一度升至 4.115%。成熟市場長債同步承壓，反映財政擴張、通膨風險與貨幣政策正常
化正共同推升長端期限溢酬。

不過，信用市場整體仍相對穩定，截至 8/24，聖路易聯準銀行的 ICE 美國銀行美國投資級公司
債 OAS 約 81 個基點、有效殖利率約 5.4%，但信用利差仍處歷史偏低區間，緩衝並不寬裕。AI 基礎
建設融資的供給壓力亦已開始反映在定價，Reuters 8/21 指出，2026 年至 8 月上旬主要 AI 雲端業
者發債規模已約 2,200 億美元，科技類投資級債利差升至 89 個基點，較整體 IG 市場高出約 9 個基
點；近期 Alphabet 新券亦需提供約 10～15 個基點讓利，顯示在企業基本面仍穩健之際，大量新券
供給已成為影響信用評價的重要因素。
展望 9 月，美債殖利率預料仍將處於相對高檔，若就業持續降溫且通膨未再明顯升高，中期利率
上行空間可望受限。期限配置方面，短端對 Fed 政策預期較敏感，過短存續期間亦有再投資風險；
10 年以上債券則持續承受財政與期限溢酬壓力。值得注意的是，截至 8/27，10 年與 30 年期殖利率
利差為 52 個基點，2 年至 30 年總利差 96 個基點，顯示 2 年至 30 年期殖利率曲線陡峭幅度中，逾

半來自 10~30 年區間，反映超長端相對 10 年期仍有明顯期限溢酬。相較之下，3～10 年期兼具較
高息收、適度存續期間與利率下行參與空間。整體而言，目前 3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風
險報酬條件相對均衡，料具表現機會。

顯著改善，不過因 N2 的出貨量快速大幅成長，有利未來整體
毛利率的改善。

將擴充一倍，而後段的 Interposer 則與 Global  Foundry 進行
合作。  
⚫ AI五年的 CAGR >55%，不含 Agentic AI 所驅動的 CPU強勁
需求，顯見 AI的需求遠優於預期。  
⚫ 我們將 2026/2027 年的稅後 EPS由 106.63/142.76 元，上修
至 111.36/152.74 元。

一. 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率仍受 N2影響  
3Q26 AI因 AI持續帶動先進製程需求強勁， N3營收比重由 2Q26
的 30%上升至 33%，N2營收比重也由 2Q26 的 3%上升至 8%，
帶動台積電 3Q26 營收達 14,942 億元， QoQ +17.62 %，
YoY +50.95 %，在成本改善、高產能利用率 及製程的升級帶動下，
抵銷 2nm量產初期對毛利率的負影響下， 3Q26毛利率 66.41%，
達公司展望的上緣， 稅後 EPS 2 9.43元，優於預期。 4Q26在 AI
帶動的先進製程及先進封裝需求持續強勁下，預估 以美元對新台
幣 1:31.8 為基礎計算，預估 營收中 值約 16,949 億元，
QoQ+13.4 3%，YoY+ 62.02 %，符合市場預期，惟 仍受 N2量產良
率調整問題的影響，預估 4Q26 整體毛利率維持在 66%左右，，
單季稅後 EPS 32.81元，續創新高 。

二. AI需求強勁，將擴大投資及合作  
由於客戶及客戶的客戶持續釋出 強勁的需求，AI 需求 5 年(2025
～2030)的 CAGR將超越 55%，且此一數字不含 Agentic AI 所帶
動的 CPU需求，主因 CPU主要的應用除了 AI之外，亦應用於傳
統的 PC及非 AI領域，顯見 AI需求的延續性拉長且需求優於預
期，台積電將積極擴產滿足客戶需求，雖然 2026年資本支出維持
600～640億美元，但因持續的海內外擴產加上 2030 年在成本合
理及正面效益下， 會開始導入 Hi-NA EUV，因此，預估未來數年
的資本支出將大幅走高。

AI帶動 GPU、ASIC需求持續成長，但進入 Agentic AI 則是帶動
CPU需求強勁成長，驅動半導體榮景延續。由於目前需求遠遠大
過台積電所能供應的產能，且持續朝先進製程應用，因此，台積電
目前 N2已在新竹、高雄量產，且 2027年產能將會擴增一倍，應
用於智慧型手機、 HPC、AI，且 A14 將會在 2027 年進行風險性
試產，2028年量產； N3方面也會擴增三個新廠，台南廠的進度符
合預期， 1H27可量產，亞利桑那州第二個 Fab(N3)在 3Q27也可
開始量產 (比預期提前 )，熊本廠於 2028年裝機量產。 由於台積電
美國大客戶及美國政府 希望台積電在美擴大產能，因此，台積電將
會在亞利桑那州以外的德州擴產，會由台積電自已營運 Fab廠，
不過目前尚未有正式結論。 雖然看似擴充相當大的產能，但台積電
都已由上而上的評估且有紀律的擴產，在電力等關鍵指標方面也
都已考慮進去了。

除了先進製程需求強勁外，先進封裝的需求也相對緊俏，因此，台
積電在後段將與 Global Foundry 合作，由 Global Foundry 進行
Interposer 的生產，以滿足客戶需求 ，台積電則是衝刺利潤率更好
的先進製程。

三. 上修 2026/2027年稅後 EPS至 111.36 /152.74元 
2026年受惠 AI及 Agentic AI 需求超乎  預期的帶動下，台積電將
2026年全年的營收由 YoY+40%上修至 44%(以美元計價 )，因此，
我們預估台積電 2026 年營收 55,936 億元，YoY+46.85%，毛利
率 66.55%，稅後淨利 28,877 億元，YoY+68.1%，稅後 EPS由
106.63 元上修至 111.36元。2027年在 GPU、ASIC、CPU、XPU
需求持續強勁的帶動下， 台積電營運仍會是強勁成長的一年，我們
預估台積電 2027 年營收 77,218 億元， YoY+37.17%，毛利率
67.53%，稅後 EPS由 142.76 元上修至 152.74 元。

四. 投資建議維持買進，目標價 3055元 
在 AI需求強勁的帶動下， 先進製程產能持續緊俏，台積電除了積
極擴擴 N3產能外， 2027 年 N2的產能將增加一倍 ，在高階產品
應用上將取得高市佔率， 且在毛利率表現方面，因 先進製程領導地
位的穩固吸引更多訂單， 訂價策略的改變， 及良率的改善，帶動毛
利率維持高檔，帶動獲利 持續大幅成長， 因此，投資建議維持買
進，目標價 3055元(20X2027年稅後 EPS)。

3 
一.公司簡介  
台積電(2330)成立於 1987年，為專業的 Fabless 半導體廠商，為
客戶生產的晶片廣泛地被運用在各種終端市場，例如行動裝置、高
效能運算、車用電子與物聯網等。 2019年，台積電及其子公司所
擁有及管理 約當年產能超過 1,200 萬片十二吋晶圓。在台灣設有
三座十二吋超大晶圓廠、四座八吋晶圓廠和一座六吋晶圓廠，並擁
有一家 100%持有之海外子公司 —台積電（南京）有限公司之十二
吋晶圓廠及二家 100%持有之海外子公司 —WaferTech 美國子公
司、台積電 (中國)有限公司之八吋晶圓廠。 台積電於 2011年第四
季開始導入 28nm，且開始加大先進製程技術的投資，於 2H12以
平面電晶體結構為基礎，在 20nm導入加強版的 HKMG 等技術，
並於 2014年進一步量產 16nm FinFET( 鰭式場效電晶體 )，持續推
進摩爾定律，儘管摩爾定律在 3nm以下將面臨物理極限，台積電
透過先進封裝製程延長摩爾定律，為高端產品 /客戶提供最先進的
技術。為因應美國製造優先及半導在在地化製造的需求，台積電於
2021年開始在美國亞利桑那州建廠，預計 興建六座先進製程廠、
2座先進封裝廠、 1個 R&D中心， 4nm已於 4Q24開始生產，預
估 3nm 也將在 2026 年進行量產，並且因為 AI 需求強勁的帶動
下，將加速 2nm及 16A的進廠速度，預估有機會於 2027～2028
年開始陸續量產。

二.營運概況及展望  
1、發展矽光子技術解決資料傳輸速度不足及訊耗損失問題  
10/2022 ChatGPT 推出後，其廣泛的推理能力，與使用者在各領
域以文字內容的方式對答如流，帶給使用者前所未有的體驗與 驚
豔，使用者呈現爆炸 性的成長，推升生成式 AI快速發展，使得 AI 
Server 呈現供不應求的現象 ，其不論在外觀的設計架構抑或 內部
的零組件規格甚至資料傳輸的頻寬及速度均較一般傳輸的 Server
大幅提升，且內部各元件的規格須同步提升才能使 AI Server 的強
大效能發揮最大的效用。

過往 Server 內部資料的傳輸採用銅線進行傳輸， 由於 AI Server
對資料傳輸的頻寬及傳輸速度的需求大幅提升， 銅線傳輸將產生
較大的訊號減損，使資料傳輸的品質不佳， 而以光做為資料傳輸的
解決方案，可解決資料傳輸訊號減損的問題， 因此，銅退光進的解
決方案勢在必行 。

矽光子的技術為銅退光進的最終解決方案， 但礙於技術及良率尚
不成熟，因此， CPO將為先行發展的技術，以 作為逐步達成光通
訊元件與主晶片直接在矽晶圓製程的階段即同時完成製作 的過渡
技術。在矽光子製作的過程中將以光波導取代 傳統銅線的傳輸，由

於光會沿著折射率較大的 物質前進，空氣的折射率為 1、水的折射
率為 1.33、玻璃的折射率為 1.46、二氧化矽的折射率為 1.6、矽
的折射率為 3.5，因此，光波導將選擇從矽晶圓上製作， 使光能進
行最佳的全反射，讓光能在光波導中的傳 送達最大的效率。由於矽
光子的發展為半導體未來的趨勢，大多數晶圓廠已投入研發，台積
電在此一趨勢下亦不缺席， 進行 40nm～90nm製程的矽光子技術
開發，預估 2025 年將利用 65nm 製程完成緊湊型通用光子引擎
(COUPE)的開發，支援可插拔式的連接器， 2026 年整合 CoWoS
封裝，將 CPO 與 Switch 晶片共同整合於同一基板上，完成共同
封裝光學 (CPO)的開發，未來將進一步將 CPO 與 XPU 共同整合
於矽仲介板上 ，實現晶片間更快的傳輸速度及較低的延時性。

圖一、矽光子發展示 意圖

資料來源： imec；宏遠投顧整理

2、透過 2.5D/3D 封裝延續摩爾定律  
矽為半導體重要材料，矽 1.0為平面式電晶 (Planar FET) 體時代，
由於電子遷移速度受到限制，製程節點 /閘極長度走到大約 28nm；
在 28nm以下進入到矽 2.0，為了改善電子元件漏電的問題， Intel
自 22nm 開始將平面世電晶體改成鰭式場效電晶體 (FinFET)，台
積電亦自 16nm開始將製程改為 FinFET，但當製程節點不斷往下
微縮至 5nm、3nm時，FinFET 將面臨摩爾定律的物理極限，即進
入矽 3.0時代，利用 2.5D及 3D封裝的技術可延續摩爾定律，台
積電在 2.5D封裝方面推出 CoWoS，Intel 推出 EMIB。CoWoS 在
晶片及基板之間加入 Si Interposer( 不具導電功能 )做為連接橋樑；
而 Intel 的 EMIB 則利用矽橋 (Silicon Bridge ；具有元件連通距離
短及反應速度快的特性 )做為多晶片之間連接的橋樑。

圖二、2.5D封裝示意圖

資料來源： Intel & 宏遠投顧彙整

在 3D封裝方面，台積電推出 3D Fabric 平台，Intel 推出 Foveros 
3D封裝架構。台積電 3D Fabric，主要為前段製程採用 Wafer on 
Wafer 及 Chip on Wafer 的 SoIC(系統整合單晶片 )方式，將晶片
進行 3D堆疊，然後結合後段先進封裝製程，包括 CoWoS及InFO，
將邏輯晶片及記憶體晶片進行 2.5D及 3D的封裝製程，達到縮小
IC 面積及提高效能的效果。而 Intel 的 Foveros 將不同製程節點
的晶片進行 3D封裝，包括 10nm製程的運算晶片、 22nm製程的
系統 I/O 晶片和 PoP(Package -on-Package)封裝的 DRAM 記憶
體。

圖三、3D封裝示意圖

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀 
 2Q26 毛利率 25.87%，低於預期，主要仍是中國毛利率拖累，2Q26 
EPS 2.02 元:迅得 2Q26 EPS 2.02 元，與元富預估 2 元差異不大。
2Q26 營收 18.24 億元， +5%QOQ，+12%YOY，2Q26 毛利 率
25.87%，低於元富預估的 26.5%，主要是中國地區仍有低價競爭影
響，本業獲利達成率 99%，業外收益挹注下，2Q26 EPS 2.02 元。 
 交期拉長，2027 年預計擴產 40%，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元:載板
客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極
布局轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，由於載板面積增大， 技術難度提
升，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。晶圓製造及
OSAT 隨客戶台灣及海外積極擴產， 交期過去 4-6 個月現在已經拉長
到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元， 預計2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 220 元：3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長，新產
能逐步開出， 故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 
11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)，目標價 220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

迅得為自動化設備供應商 
 迅得為自動化設備供應商 
迅得機械成立於 1999 年，主要產品為印刷電路板、軟板、光電及半導體物流系
統之自動化解決方案，2025 年營收比重可區分為 PCB 事業部(41%)、半導體-
載板事業部 13%、半導體-晶圓事業部 12%、半導體-封裝事業部 32%，面板事
業部 2%。主要客戶包括欣興、南亞、景碩、台郡、臻鼎、日月光、台積電、友
達、群創等。

個股報告

載板 、 晶圓客戶 、OSAT 客戶均樂
觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元 
迅得 1H26 營收占比為 PCB 4 1%，載板 16%，晶圓製造 16%，封裝 25%，其
他 2%。PCB 主要產品以自動上下料、物件搬運及智慧倉儲為主； IC 載板亦為
自動上下料較多，整廠規畫約佔比 60%，單機搬運 20%，倉儲 15~20%；半導
體晶圓廠則以倉儲為主，台灣部分標準倉儲較多，微型倉儲較少， 海外部分目
前未出貨標準倉儲，僅供應微型倉儲，主要是安裝時間及人力配置考量；封裝
OSAT 廠主要供應倉儲佔比 60~70%，自動化搬運約 30%。

載板客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極布局
轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，以欣興資本支出來看，2026 年 340 億元，2027
年長交期採購設備訂單金額 92 億元 ， 均將投入桃園楊梅廠及光復廠 ； 景碩2026
年資本支出 140 億元(原本是 80 億元)，將投入於六廠建置及設備導入，2027
年資本支出為 160 億元(原本是 100 億元)， 擴產進度明確 。 由於載板面積增大，
技術難度提升 ，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出 ，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。

晶圓製造部分，隨 客戶台灣及海外積極擴產，因目前現有自動化廠商均無單一
廠商可獨立供足客戶所需 自動化設備量體，故每個案件 公司均有相對應配比，
以海外廠來說，考量人力成本及安裝時間公司僅提供微型倉儲為主。 OSAT 部
分，客戶拉貨持續積極，半導體業務維持樂觀。以交期來看，過去 4~6 個月現
在已經拉長到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元，預計 2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

維持買進，目標價 220 元

維持買進評等，目標價 220 元 
3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長 ， 新產能逐步開出 ， 故維持買進 ， 預估2026 年 EPS 
8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)， 目標價220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

## Document 237: Anthropic推出全天候AI智能体Claude Duet，Opus 5.5 驱动

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic推出全天候AI智能体Claude Duet，Opus 5.5 驱动.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 表⽰， Claude Duet ⾯向⼯作任务繁重的专业⼈⼠和⼩企业主设计，定位为能够
在后台持续运⾏、主动识别⽤⼾需求并执⾏⼯作任务的 AI 智能体。⽬前产品仍处于研究预
览阶段，仅通过候补名单向美国地区的 Claude Pro 和 Max 订阅⽤⼾开放。
摘要：
Anthropic 正将 AI 竞争从模型能⼒延伸⾄任务执⾏。
当地时间周三，公司推出新应⽤ Claude Duet ，将其定位为能够在后台持续运⾏、主动识别⽤⼾
需求并执⾏⼯作任务的 AI 智能体，进⼀步拓展 Claude 的产品形态。
与等待⽤⼾发出指令的传统对话式助⼿不同， Claude Duet 能够汇总关联应⽤中的重要信息，在
需要⽤⼾关注时主动发送通知，并处理从会议安排到财务预测、⽹⻚应⽤开发等多种任务。
不过，这款产品⽬前仍处于研究预览阶段，仅通过候补名单向美国地区的 Claude Pro 和 Max 订
阅⽤⼾开放。有限的开放范围意味着， Claude Duet 尚未进⼊⾯向更⼴泛⽤⼾群体的全⾯竞争阶

段。
Anthropic 表⽰， Claude Duet 基于其旗舰模型 Claude Opus 5.5 开发，可在⽤⼾离开设备后继续
执⾏任务，但在判断信息紧急程度和处理部分个⼈事务⽅⾯仍存在局限。随着 Meta 和 OpenAI 也
在推进主动式 AI 智能体， Anthropic 正尝试将模型能⼒转化为⽤⼾服务。
从响应指令转向主动执⾏
根据 Anthropic 发布的官⽅公告， Claude Duet ⾯向⼯作任务繁重的专业⼈⼠和⼩企业主设计。
应⽤每天早晨会汇总关联应⽤中的重要事项，并在⼀天中持续检查相关信息，在发现需要⽤⼾
处理的事项时主动发出提醒。
⽤⼾也可以直接交办任务。除安排会议等⽇常⾏政⼯作外， Claude Duet 还能够借助多个⼯具构
建仪表盘、制作财务或销售预测、开发原型及⽹⻚应⽤。
其核⼼变化在于，⽤⼾不必始终停留在对话界⾯中逐步下达指令，智能体可以在后台推进任
务，并在完成或需要审核时通知⽤⼾。
Anthropic 称， Claude Duet 继承⽤⼾ Claude 账⼾中的记忆、技能和已连接应⽤，减少重复配置

的需要。产品提供独⽴的 iOS 和 Android 应⽤，也可通过⽹⻚端使⽤。
依托 Opus 5.5 ，连接逾 6000 种⼯具
Claude Duet 以 Claude Opus 5.5 为基础，并配备独⽴虚拟机和浏览器环境，使其能够在⽤⼾的
电脑或⼿机关闭后继续⼯作。
根据 Anthropic 的介绍，产品可以调⽤ Claude Marketplace 中超过 6000 种连接器和插件，覆盖
Gmail 、 Slack 等⼯作⼯具，以及 QuickBooks 、 Stripe 等业务服务。
这些连接器使智能体能够将信息检索与后续操作结合起来。例如，它可以检查未处理的邮件和
未付款发票、起草提醒信息、准备薪资处理内容，也可以协助制作营销材料、管理⽹店或准备
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
286 收藏 分享到 :
合同⽂件。
⽀付和信息发送等关键步骤仍受到审批机制约束。 Anthropic 表⽰， Claude Duet 在发送消息、付
款、签署⽂件或删除信息前，会向⽤⼾展⽰拟执⾏的操作并等待批准。
产品也设置了专⻔的安全机制，以防⽌敏感信息被不当披露；部分⾼⻛险操作，即使获得⽤⼾
批准也⽆法执⾏。
研究预览阶段，能⼒与使⽤范围仍有限
Claude Duet ⽬前并⾮全⾯开放的商业产品。 Anthropic 此次⾸先向美国地区的 Pro 和 Max 订阅⽤
⼾开放候补申请，研究预览期间，智能体与 Claude 其他功能共享订阅使⽤额度，后台检查和复
杂任务同样会消耗额度。
Anthropic 也承认，现阶段产品仍可能误读对话内容或错误判断事项的紧急程度。由于产品主要
围绕⼯作场景开发，其处理订票等个⼈事务的能⼒相对较弱，⽤⼾⽬前也⽆法直接查看其浏览

器操作过程。
因此， Claude Duet 的实际价值仍取决于它能否在持续获取信息、主动判断任务优先级和执⾏操
作之间形成稳定的⼯作流程。对于企业和专业⽤⼾⽽⾔，后台⾃动化有望减少信息筛选和重复
操作，但判断失误与敏感信息处理仍是使⽤过程中需要关注的问题。
AI 智能体竞争进⼀步延伸
Anthropic 推出 Claude Duet 之际， OpenAI 和 Meta 也在推进能够代表⽤⼾执⾏任务的 AI 智能体。
OpenAI 此前推出了 Dots ， Meta 则推出了 Muse ，相关产品均试图让 AI 从回答问题进⼀步⾛向跨
应⽤完成任务。
The Verge 将 Claude Duet 视为 Meta Muse 和 OpenAI Dots 的竞争对⼿，并指出， Duet 整合了
Claude ⽣态中的记忆、技能与应⽤连接能⼒，通过虚拟机和浏览器执⾏⼯作任务。
Business Insider 则指出， Claude Duet ⽬前仍处于候补名单开放阶段，尚不能⽴即⾯向更⼴泛
的市场与其他智能体产品展开直接竞争。

对 Anthropic ⽽⾔， Claude Duet 不仅是 Claude 应⽤形态的扩展，也意味着公司正在探索如何让
模型持续参与⽤⼾的⽇常⼯作，⽽不只是提供⼀次性的问答和内容⽣成服务。其后续发展将取
决于任务执⾏的可靠性、⽤⼾对主动式服务的接受程度，以及产品能否从有限的研究预览逐步
扩⼤使⽤范围。

## Document 277: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to

The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

Introduction 
With trillions of dollars, euros and yen changing hands every day, foreign 
exchange is indisputably the largest and most liquid financial market in the 
world. Yet despite its size, the currency market is also one of the least 
understood. Unlike bonds or equities, there is no widely accepted framework for 
explaining currency moves, which reflect a complex interplay of 
macroeconomics, capital flows, market structure, investor behaviour and 
(geo)politics. 
Over the past two decades, advances in technology and market infrastructure 
have transformed how currencies are traded, placing FX at the forefront of 
broader changes in global finance. Yet because currencies sit at the intersection 
of national economies, the FX market is also fundamentally connected to 
international trade and cross-border capital flows. It is therefore increasingly 
exposed to the forces reshaping the global economic landscape—from 
geopolitical fragmentation and changing trade relationships to shifts in 
regulation, payments infrastructure and the architecture of the international 
monetary system. 
This Deutsche Bank Currency Book brings together academic, industry and 
Deutsche Bank expertise to explain how FX markets function, how exchange 
rates are determined, how investors seek to generate returns from currencies, 
and how FX risk can be managed by investors and corporates alike. Our aim 
throughout the Book is to set out the enduring principles driving markets 
alongside the developments defining FX today. Ultimately, the structure of the FX 
market is not simply a question of how currencies trade, but of who controls the 
financial infrastructure through which money moves across borders. The 
evolution of FX is increasingly intertwined with questions of monetary 
sovereignty, financial resilience and geopolitical power. 
Chapter 1 reviews the structure of the FX market, examining its participants, 
ecosystem and evolution over time. Chapter 2 discusses exchange rate 
determination, moving from traditional academic theories to modern macro-
financial approaches and practical decision frameworks. Chapter 3 reviews FX 
as an asset class and the evidence on currency investing, while Chapter 4 
considers currency hedging for both investors and corporates. Finally, Chapter 5 
discusses the future of FX, including the impact of technology, payment systems 
and digital assets.

Introduction

is indisputably the largest and most liquid financial market in the world. Yet despite

### Page 7

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
Introduction
The foreign exchange market is the world’s largest and most liquid financial market,
but it differs fundamentally from a centralised exchange. Trading takes place across
a fragmented, largely over-the-counter network of banks, non-bank liquidity
providers, electronic venues and end-users, using instruments ranging from spot
and forwards to swaps, futures and options. How do transactions move through

this network? Who provides liquidity, who consumes it and what risks arise
between execution and settlement? This chapter maps the FX ecosystem from
instruments and trading infrastructure to the participants ultimately responsible for
currency demand.
1.1 Instruments
The FX market is not one single product. Instead, it is made up of a group of
instruments that allow market participants to exchange currencies today, lock in an
exchange rate for the future and/or hedge against adverse exchange-rate moves.

Below, we outline the main instruments in FX markets and their users.
Spot: This is the simplest and most widely known FX instrument. When market
participants refer to “the exchange-rate” or the “spot exchange-rate,” they
generally mean the price agreed today for exchanging one currency for another on
the standard spot settlement date. For most major currency pairs spot settlement
is two business days later (T+2).1  For example, a spot purchase of EUR 1 million

against the USD at a EUR/USD exchange-rate of 1.20 means that the buyer receives
EUR 1 million in his or her bank account on the settlement date and the seller
receives USD 1.2 million from the buyer on the same date.
1 There are exceptions, for example USD/CAD settles T+1.

### Page 8

The Currency Book
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
By convention, the first currency in an exchange-rate quotation is known as the
base currency, while the second is the quote currency. The exchange-rate therefore
represents the number of units of the quote currency required to purchase one unit
of the base currency. In USD/JPY, for example, the USD is the base currency and the
exchange-rate indicates the number of Japanese yen required to purchase one US

dollar. A USD/JPY exchange-rate of 160 means 1 USD can be exchanged for 160
JPY.
Spot transactions are the most commonly used foreign exchange payments for
cross-border payments for goods and services, the purchase of foreign financial
assets, as well as portfolio rebalancing and proprietary trading. In 2025, the Bank
for International Settlements (BIS) estimates spot turnover at $3trn per day,
accounting for 31% of global over-the-counter (OTC) FX turnover.

Forwards and futures: An (outright) forward is similar to a spot transaction, but is
instead an agreement to exchange two currencies at a rate (price) agreed today, but
with settlement taking place at a date more than T+2 days in the future. They are
also traded OTC. Forwards are most commonly used by those who have a known
future cash flow and want to remove uncertainty around the future exchange rate.
In 2025, the BIS estimates that they account for 19% of global turnover, or $1.8trn

per day.
Futures are economically similar to forwards but are standardised contracts traded
on exchanges and centrally cleared. This provides transparent pricing and reduces
bilateral counterparty risk, but offers less flexibility over contract size, maturity and
settlement terms than an OTC forward. Exchange-traded FX futures remain
considerably smaller than the OTC FX market.
Non-deliverable forwards (NDFs): An NDF is a cash-settled forward commonly

used for currencies where convertibility or offshore deliverability is restricted. Two
parties agree today on an exchange rate for a future date, but unlike a standard
forward, the underlying currencies are not exchanged. At maturity, the agreed NDF
rate is compared with a pre-specified fixing or reference rate. The difference is
usually settled in USD. NDFs are commonly used by corporates, banks and
investors to hedge or take exposure to currencies subject to capital controls.

Swaps: An FX swap combines two transactions: an exchange of currencies on an
initial date and a reverse exchange at a later date, with both exchange rates agreed
when the swap is initiated. They are used by institutions that hold cash in one
currency (e.g. EUR) but need funding in another (e.g. USD) for investment, hedging
or other needs. They are by far the largest FX instrument by turnover, with the BIS
estimating turnover of $4trn per day in April 2025, 42% of global FX turnover. A

swap is, in effect, a form of collateralised borrowing, with the collateral being cash
in a different currency to that of the currency borrowed.
Cross-currency (XCCY) swaps: A cross-currency swap is similar to an FX swap in
that principal amounts denominated in two different currencies are typically
exchanged at inception and reversed at maturity. The key difference is that a cross-
currency swap also involves periodic exchanges of interest payments denominated

in the two currencies over the life of the contract. These payments may be fixed or
floating. Cross-currency swaps therefore allow market participants to transform
both the currency denomination and, where relevant, the interest-rate
characteristics of an asset or liability.

### Page 9

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
For example:
n Suppose a European corporate issues a USD-denominated bond to US-
based investors. It receives the bond proceeds in USD, but must
periodically pay interest in USD and repay the USD principal when the bond
matures. However, because the corporate primarily earns revenue in EUR,
it needs to convert these USD liabilities into EUR liabilities to avoid currency
and potentially interest-rate risk.

n The corporate removes FX and interest-rate risk by entering into a xccy
swap, where, in essence, it converts this USD-liability into a EUR-liability.2
n They are commonly used by banks, corporates and investors that need to
raise money in one currency but need funds in another. The basis is the extra
spread added to one side of the swap to balance unequal demand for
funding in the two currencies. See Section 2.11 for more on cross-currency
basis and here.

Options: An option gives the holder the right, but not the obligation, to buy or sell
one currency against another at a specified exchange rate during a specified period.
Options are used to hedge currency risk or speculate on currency movements and
are particularly useful when the end-user wants an asymmetric payoff profile.
Unlike a forward, an option can limit losses from an adverse currency movement
while allowing the holder to retain some or all of the benefit from a favourable

movement. In 2025, they represented around 7% of global FX turnover. Options are
widely used by corporates and investors for hedging, and by investors and dealers
to express directional or volatility views.
The simplest and most widely used instruments are vanilla options, which give the
holder the right to buy (a call) or sell (a put) a currency at a predetermined strike
price. A European-style option can be exercised only at expiry, while an American-

style option can be exercised at any point up to expiry. More structured products
include barrier options, whose payoff is activated or extinguished if the exchange
rate reaches a specified level—for example, knock-in and knock-out options. One-
touch and no-touch options pay a fixed amount depending on whether the
exchange-rate does, or does not, reach a predetermined level during the life of the
option. These instruments are commonly used to express views not just on the

direction of a currency pair, but also on the probability, timing and magnitude of
exchange-rate moves.
1.2 FX spot trading life cycle and risks
Having discussed the main FX instruments, the next step is to examine how these
transactions are executed and settled in practice. The complete trade life cycle,
meaning the sequence of events and processes involved in executing a trade, is
crucial to understanding how FX transactions work. We therefore describe below

the broad steps involved in trading FX, focusing on spot transactions. They are:
1.  Pre-trade: This stage encompasses all the necessary preparations before
a trade is executed, including client onboarding, platform selection, credit
checks, and regulatory compliance.
2 Specifically, it exchanges the USD bond proceeds for EUR at inception, receives USD interest payments
(from the swap counterparty to pay U.S. bond investors) and pays EUR interest (to the swap

counterparty) over the life of the swap, and receives USD principal (from the swap counterparty to pay
U.S. investors) in exchange for EUR principal at maturity. The USD receipts from the swap are used to
meet the bond’s USD interest and principal payments, effectively converting the USD-denominated
bond into a EUR-denominated liability.

### Page 10

The Currency Book
Page 10 Deutsche Bank AG
2.  Trade execution: This is the point where the trade is agreed upon and
executed at a specific exchange rate, creating a legally binding contract.
3.  Trade clearing: This stage involves validating trade details and managing
counterparty credit risk before settlement, either bilaterally or through a
central clearing counterparty (CCP). In centrally cleared FX markets, the
CCP becomes the buyer to every seller and the seller to every buyer,

reducing direct counterparty exposures between market participants.
Specialist FX CCPs, such as LCH's ForexClear, clear both non-deliverable
and deliverable FX products. While FX spot transactions can be cleared,
they are not subject to mandatory clearing requirements. ForexClear
supports 25 non-deliverable forward (NDF) currency pairs, 9 non-
deliverable option (NDO) currency pairs, and 8 deliverable (Spot, Forwards
and Options) currency pairs, at the time of writing.

4.  Trade settlement: This final stage represents the completion of the
transaction, with the actual exchange of currencies taking place.
Settlement for spot trades typically occurs two business days (T+2) after
the trade date, although exceptions exist. For example, USD/CAD settles
on a T+1 basis.
Figure 2: FX Spot Trade Life Cycle
Source : Deutsche Bank
Risks surrounding FX spot trading
The over-the-counter (OTC) nature of the FX market, while offering flexibility and

adaptability to participant needs, does present certain risks that are often more
pronounced compared to exchange-traded markets. Below we discuss some of the
key risks in FX.
Credit risk: Credit risk in FX trading, stemming from a counterparty's potential
inability to fulfill its obligations, is present throughout the trade lifecycle:
n Pre-trade: Counterparty creditworthiness is a critical consideration in the
A Central Clearing Counterparty (CCP), also

known as a Central Counterparty, stands
between buyers and sellers in financial
markets, becoming the buyer to every
seller and the seller to every buyer. This
mitigates counterparty risk.

### Page 11

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
pre-trade stage of FX transactions. A thorough assessment should involve
evaluating the potential counterparty's financial strength, credit rating, and
reputation. Choosing a creditworthy counterparty significantly reduces the
risk of default throughout the trade lifecycle.
n Post-trade (Clearing and Settlement): While credit risk exists in both the
clearing (post-execution/pre-settlement) and settlement stages of a spot

FX trade, it primarily materializes during settlement. This stage, involving
the actual exchange of currencies, carries the highest risk due to the
potential for settlement failure. This risk, often referred to as settlement risk,
arises when one party delivers their currency while the counterparty
defaults. Cross-border trades are particularly vulnerable due to time zone
differences, which can create a window where one party has performed but
the other has not. Additionally, mark-to-market risk from FX rate

fluctuations between trade and settlement dates can exacerbate
settlement risk by increasing potential losses from a default. This is
particularly pronounced with forward contracts, where the longer pre-
settlement period leads to greater mark-to-market risk and, consequently,
higher settlement risk.
Market Risk: This risk in FX trading arises from potential losses due to adverse
exchange-rate movements. This is influenced by factors like economic data,

geopolitical events, and interest rates. For customers, asymmetric information,
where one party has more or better information than another, can lead to inefficient
pricing and increased volatility, thereby exacerbating market risk.
n For dealers, a key component of market risk is inventory management risk
– the potential for losses from unfavourable price movements in the
currencies they hold to facilitate client trading. Effective inventory
management, including position limits and hedging, mitigates this risk.

Liquidity Risk: This refers to the overall market's ability to facilitate the buying and
selling of a currency pair quickly and efficiently at a desired price. It's influenced by
factors such as market depth, trading volume, volatility, and fragmentation.
Operational Risk: This encompasses risks associated with trade processing,
settlement, and technology. Errors in trade execution, system failures, and fraud
can all lead to significant losses.
Regulatory Risk: This refers to the risk arising from changes in regulations or their

interpretation. The evolving regulatory landscape in FX, particularly concerning
non-bank dealers and systemic risk, can impact market participants and create
uncertainty.
1.3 Market participants
The FX market comprises a variety of participants with different objectives. FX
market participants fall into two broad categories: financial and non-financial.
Financial participants can act as both liquidity providers and consumers, while non-
financial participants act largely as liquidity consumers.

Financial participants: In the FX market, financial participants are primarily banks
acting as dealers.
n Dealers: These are mainly large commercial banks, investment banks and
securities houses that participate in the inter-dealer market for proprietary

### Page 12

The Currency Book
Page 12 Deutsche Bank AG
trading. 3 and/or for meeting customer demand through market-making.4
n Institutional investors: These are mutual funds, pension funds, insurance
companies and endowment funds, collectively referred to as “real-money
investors”, which need to buy currencies to satisfy end-user demand for an
underlying asset. For instance, a UK pension fund needs to buy USD to
purchase US equities. These investors also engage in hedging, proprietary

trading and risk management activities.
n Hedge Funds & Principal Trading Firms (PTFs): Hedge funds are
investment funds that follow a broad range of investment strategies and are
generally not subject to the same borrowing and leverage restrictions as
traditional investment funds. They are users of FX and derivatives for
speculative purposes. PTFs are principal trading firms, such as XTX
Markets and Citadel Securities, that hedge or speculate for their own

account and also participate in market-making. This category may include
high-frequency trading firms that employ high-speed algorithmic trading
strategies.
Non financial institutions: Non-financial participants, such as corporations
involved in international trade, retail and the official sector, also primarily consume
liquidity.
n Corporates are active in currency markets primarily to manage FX
exposures 5  arising from international trade, foreign-currency financing,

overseas operations and cross-border mergers and acquisitions (M&A).6
n Official sector: This category includes central banks, sovereign wealth
funds, international financial institutions, such as the IMF and BIS,
development banks and government agencies. Monetary-policy, financial-
stability and mercantilist considerations have all, at various times,
motivated central-bank intervention in FX.7
n Other: This category includes all other market participants, including retail

participants. Retail participants engage in FX both to speculate and to meet
transactional demand arising from travel, trade or investment.
1.4 Profit versus non-profit seekers
In 2025, daily FX turnover reached $9.5 trillion according to the BIS triennial survey
(BIS, 2025) making currency trading the world's largest financial market. Yet,
size and liquidity do not necessarily imply efficiency. Unlike most equity or
bond transactions, a large share of FX activity serves an underlying

transactional purpose. For instance, corporates must convert revenues and
meet foreign- currency invoices; institutional investors hedge overseas
assets; and dealers intermediate these flows. For these participants,
expected FX returns may be secondary, or even irrelevant, to the decision to
trade. In this section, we investigate
3 Proprietary trading is a profit-seeking activity that dealers once engaged in more extensively, but which
has been severely constrained by regulatory change. Most notably, the Volcker Rule, implemented in

2014-15 under the Dodd-Frank regulations, bans banks from engaging in proprietary trading activities,
including many foreign exchange instruments.
4 Market making: This is an activity that seeks to capture returns by matching buyers and sellers and
charging a price for the service through the bid-offer spread. This spread represents the price that
liquidity seekers must pay to access the market and is a “frictional” cost of executing transactions. As

such, market-making is a liquidity-provision service rather than a source of the ‘excess returns'
associated with activities such as proprietary trading
Institutional investors are subject to a
variety of (1) regulatory, (2) mandate and
(3) process constraints.
(1) Regulatory constraints have had an
increasingly important influence on cross-
border investment since 2008.
(2) Mandate constraints relate to limits
placed on investment allocations by a

fund’s internal rules. These constraints may
arise from, among other things, fund
design (i.e., fund characteristics, regions,
target benchmarks, target returns),
founding charters or guidelines (i.e. many
endowments have externally imposed
constraints on investment allocations and
government policy (i.e. Norway's pension
fund mandate is determined by the
government). These restrictions can
materially limit a fund’s ability to take
currency risk. The simplest mandate

distinction is between passive and active
funds, with the former following an index
and therefore being explicitly required to
track its returns.
(3) Process constraints relate to limits
placed on the decision-making process.
Most investment funds abide by internal
risk rules designed to limit exposure to
losses during periods of market volatility.
These rules can generate value-at-risk (VaR)
shocks.
5 For more see here
6 Corporates using foreign exchange to hedge future exposures may also seek hedge accounting to

### Page 13

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 13
the division of the market between profit-seeking and non-profit-seeking
participants. This is the first step in understanding exchange-rate determination.
Figure 3: Daily FX turnover by counterparty
Source : BIS, Deutsche bank
Figure 4: Daily FX turnover by instrument
Source : BIS, Deutsche Bank
Approach 1: The Triennial BIS Survey of FX market volumes
We use the 2025 BIS Triennial Survey to update Deutsche Bank's previous work

(Saravelos et al, 2018) and estimate the share of FX turnover initiated with a profit-
seeking currency objective. We construct a "realistic scenario", which applies what
we deem to be "realistic" assumptions about participants’ ability and mandate to
take FX risk.8 We find that profit-seeking activity accounts for approximately 40%
of turnover under the realistic scenario.9
Financial participants
n Dealers: Dealer-to-dealer activity accounts for approximately 47% of total

daily FX turnover ($4.44trn) and we classify 10% of dealer flow as profit-
seeking ($444bn/day). Given that much of the daily FX turnover reflects
market-making, we assume that inter-dealer transactions conducted by
voice are not profit-seeking and that only electronic transactions
conducted through order-matching systems such as EBS are profit-
seeking.
n Hedge Funds and PTFs: Hedge Funds and PTFs account for approximately
$760bn (8%) of headline daily FX turnover and we classify 90% of this as

profit-seeking ($684bn/day). The $684bn/day figure represents the profit-
seeking nature of hedge-funds where the remaining 10% reflects the
ensure that the mark-to-market value of their hedge does not introduce unwanted volatility in the
income statement. IFRS 9 provided an updated set of standards for hedge accounting, designed to
increase flexibility and better link financial reporting to firms’ risk management practices. However,
hedge accounting still places limitations on the types of instruments and kinds of hedges that can be

implemented. For example, to qualify for hedge accounting, corporates must adhere to hedge
effectiveness and demonstrate that an economic relationship between the underlying cash flow or
asset and the hedge exists, as well as quantitative limits to hedge ratios.
7 Exchange rates are explicitly targeted by some central banks as part of their overall monetary policy
framework (for instance the Monetary Authority of Singapore or the Hong Kong Monetary Authority);

other central banks have intervened to defend their inflation target or financial stability reasons (most
recently, the Swiss National Bank); and others to prevent competitiveness losses in global trade.

Approach 2: Commitment of Traders CFTC data
While the BIS data provide the most comprehensive view of the global FX market,
the survey is only updated every three years. For a higher-frequency perspective, we
therefore complement it with the US Commodity Futures Trading Commission’s
Commitments of Traders (CoT) report, which is published weekly and provides a

breakdown of positions in exchange-traded currency futures and options,
principally those traded on the Chicago Mercantile Exchange (CME). The report
sho
ws the number of outstanding long and short contracts held by different
categories of market participant, based on positions as of each Tuesday. Although
the futures market represents only a small subset of total global FX activity—most
of which takes place over the counter—the CoT data provide a timely and consistent

measure of how speculative and hedging positions are evolving.
11 Corporates use FX to settle foreign-currency revenues and expenses, finance cross-border activity,
hedge forecast cash flows and manage earnings volatility. Governments similarly transact to meet an
underlying funding or expenditure requirement.
12 Some investors trade currencies to earn returns, while other transactions relate to travel, remittances
or purchases of foreign assets

### Page 16

The Currency Book
Page 16 Deutsche Bank AG
In the traditional or “legacy” CoT report, reportable positions  are divided into
commercial and non-commercial traders, alongside a residual category of smaller,
non-reportable positions. Commercial traders are generally entities that use futures
primarily in connection  with an underlying business activity  and are therefore
commonly interpreted as hedgers—for  example, corporates or financial
institutions managing currency exposures. We classify these positions as liquidity

seeking. Non-commercial traders,  by contrast, are participants  whose futures
activity is not classified as commercial hedging and typically includes hedge funds,
commodity trading advisers and other leveraged investors. We therefore treat
non-commercial positions as profit-seeking. Figure 7 and Figure 8 show how the
relative size of the profit-seeking FX market has developed over time. Using one-
year rolling averages to reduce the noise of the data, we see that roughly 30-40%

of the futures market (EUR/USD, GBP/USD, USD/JPY) is profit-seeking.

Approach 3: Options market DTCC data
Following the implementation of the Dodd-Frank Act, OTC FX options falling within
the US regulatory perimeter are required to be reported to a CFTC-registered swap
data repository (SDR). This includes transactions involving US market participants,
among others. DTCC operates one of the largest data repositories and therefore

captures a substantial share of global institutional FX option activity. We estimate
that the DTCC data capture more than  20% of global FX derivative  volumes,
providing another way of estimating the relative importance of profit- and non-
profit-seeking activity in the FX market.
While the data does not provide  client granularity, we can back out the  likely
motivation of the transaction by distinguishing between out-of-the-money and in-
the-money option trades. We argue that strikes preferred by the different client

groups will likely differ, with hedgers favouring out of the money options (0-25
delta). While speculators focus on directional leverage, the latter often translates
to options with strikes nearer to the money (25-75 delta).
Figure 9 shows the split in options volumes for 2025/26 across the two investor
categories. We find that option volumes are roughly split equally between investors
and hedgers. FX options are instruments that are favoured by hedge funds over

corporates and institutional investors, so the results here likely materially overstate
the relative importance of profit-maximizing participants in other markets. Again,
this shows the FX market is split roughly 50/50 by those who are profit-seeking and
those who are not.

1.5 The FX ecosystem
Following Grover and Saravelos (2025) , this section examines the significant

changes in FX market structure in recent decades, with a particular focus on the FX
spot market, and highlights the key challenges that have emerged as a result of this
transformation. To understand how these challenges emerged, it is useful to begin
by examining the evolution of the FX spot market.
1.5.1 The Evolution of FX Trading: From Voice to Electronic
We analyse the evolution of the FX spot market, focusing on the shift from a pre-
electronification era of voice-based trading and bank-centric risk management to

the phased electronification of interdealer and dealer-client segments. This
transformation has led to the emergence of diverse execution styles and trading
venues, which will be explored to provide a comprehensive overview of the
changing FX landscape and its implications for risk mitigation. The discussion
begins with an examination of the pre-electronification era.
1. Voice trading: The age of the telephone
Early foreign exchange (FX) trading was a two-tiered system conducted over the

phone (Figure 10). Clients contacted dealers (outer tier) directly to inquire about bid
and ask prices. Dealers profited from the difference between these prices (the bid-
ask spread). Dealers further traded amongst themselves (the "inter-dealer market"
- inner tier) to manage inventory imbalances arising from trading with clients. They
did this either directly or anonymously through voice brokers.
In this two-tier system, banks acted as the sole liquidity providers and risk bearers

in the FX market. Inter-dealer trading, used for hedging and inventory management,
formed a significant portion of overall trading volume.

### Page 18

The Currency Book
Page 18 Deutsche Bank AG
Figure 10: The age of the telephone
Source : Deutsche Bank
2. Rise of the machines
The first wave of electronic trading in FX markets primarily affected the inter-dealer
market, replacing phone-based trading with computer systems while leaving the
dealer-client relationship unchanged. Here, we first discuss the main innovations in
the inter-dealer market and then move on to the dealer-client segment.
(a) Electronification of inter-dealer market

Two major innovations in the trading process facilitated by technological advances
led to major transformations in the inter-dealer market. They are:
n The introduction of electronic brokers, Reuters/Refinitiv (now LSEG) and
EBS, in the early 1990s brought a centralized, anonymous limit order book
to FX trading, similar to equity market exchanges. This increased the
efficiency of price discovery and inventory risk management. These two
platforms are still considered primary trading venues.

n Large banks increasingly internalized trades (settling within their own
books) in the early to mid-2000s. This reduced their need to manage
inventory risk through inter-dealer platforms. Figure 11 demonstrates this
shift in FX market dynamics, showing a decline in dealer-reported spot
trading from roughly 70% in the 1990s to 40% recently. For the largest
dealers, internalization now accounts for an estimated 80% or more of
major currency pair trades (Moore et al., 2016; Schrimpf and Sushko, 2019).

The two innovations (electronic brokers and trade internalisation) primarily
impacted the inter-dealer market, improving efficiency and narrowing bid-ask
spreads (King et. al, 2012). Thus, resulting in a more efficient inventory risk
management (Figure 12).
The benefits of electronification were not limited to the trading process. It also
helped FX markets address settlement risk in the late 1990s. Settlement risk (a
form of credit risk) , also known as "Herstatt risk," arose due to cross-border

currency transfers being limited by central bank operating hours. This created a
window where one party to a transaction could default after the other had already
settled.
Settlement risk posed a significant challenge. The potential for a default after one
leg of an FX transaction had settled created a substantial credit exposure for banks.
This exposure could be particularly large for major dealers, where transactions with

### Page 19

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 19
even a single counterparty could exceed the bank's capital.
Figure 11: Drop in dealer share of spot trading as a result
of internalization

Reporting dealers Non-dealers
Source : Deutsche Bank, BIS Triennial Survey
Figure 12: Rise of the machines Part I – electronification of
the inter-dealer
Source : Deutsche Bank
To address this, a group of banks developed a new electronic settlement system –

the Continuous Linked Settlement (CLS) Bank, which began operating in 2002.
CLS mitigates settlement risk by simultaneously settling both sides of a trade and
by netting payments between member banks.
This netting process drastically reduces the actual funds transferred for settlement
to just 4% of the total traded value.  More than $14 trillion in gross financial
obligations were settled on an average day in April 2025, with just over one-third
(36%) settled through PvP systems, predominantly CLS, eliminating FX settlement

risk for those transactions.
(b) Electronification of the dealer-client segment
Electronic trading in the inter-dealer market increased efficiency and narrowed bid-
ask spreads within that market. However, the dealer-client segment remained
largely unchanged, and did not experience the same efficiency gains and reduced
bid-ask spreads as the inter-dealer market. This resulted in a surge of new electronic
trading platforms specifically designed for clients.

With the electronification of the FX market, new liquidity providers emerged,
aiming to reduce market-related liquidity risk and reshape the trading landscape.
These new players, enabled by technological advancements, include:
1. Multi-dealer platforms (MDP), emerging in the early 2000s, allowed clients to
request for quotes (RFQs) from multiple dealers at once. This streamlined the price
discovery process, enabling clients to compare pricing from various dealers

through a single platform rather than individual phone calls. The increased
competition and transparency benefited clients. Example of MDPs include but are

### Page 20

The Currency Book
Page 20 Deutsche Bank AG
not limited to FXconnect, FXall, 360T.
2. Single dealer platforms (SDP): Many banks developed their own single-dealer
platforms (SDPs) to facilitate direct electronic trading with clients. These SDPs
largely replaced older communication methods such as telephone and telex for
bilateral trading. Examples include J.P. Morgan eXecute, UBS  Neo, and Citi
Velocity.
Financial and non-financial consumers of FX (liquidity consumers) can access both

A key distinction, however, is that MDPs primarily facilitate connections between
liquidity consumers (e.g., asset managers, hedge funds, corporations) and liquidity
providers (mainly banks). They do not bear the risk of inventory imbalances
stemming from client trades. Conversely, SDPs are integrated within individual
bank infrastructures and, consequently, are part of the bank's broader risk
management function.
3. Prime-brokerage: The emergence of prime brokerage (PB) arrangements in the

late 1990s marked a significant shift in FX market structure, allowing clients such
as hedge funds to access the inter-dealer market directly or through electronic
brokers.Under a prime-brokerage arrangement, a large bank effectively lends its
credit standing and trading relationships to a client—typically a hedge fund, asset
manager or other active non-bank institution. Rather than having to establish
separate bilateral credit lines and trading documentation with every dealer it wishes

to face, the client establishes a primary credit relationship with a single prime
broker. The prime broker sets trading limits, monitors the client's exposures,
requires collateral or margin where appropriate and charges a fee, typically linked
to trading volume and the amount of credit consumed.
This structure allows hedge funds to access liquidity that was historically available
mainly to banks in the interdealer market. A hedge fund can execute an FX trade

with one of many approved dealing banks, or through an electronic venue linking
multiple dealers, using the credit of its prime broker. The executing dealer therefore
does not need to extend a separate bilateral credit line to the hedge fund itself:
subject to pre-agreed limits, it accepts the prime broker as the ultimate credit
counterparty. Once the client executes the trade, it is “given up” to the prime broker.
In legal and settlement terms, the transaction is generally transformed into two

matching trades: one between the executing dealer and the prime broker, and
another between the prime broker and the hedge fund client. The prime broker
consequently stands between the client and the wider dealer network from a credit
perspective, even though it need not be involved in choosing the price or executing
the original trade.
Prime brokerage therefore separates execution from credit provision. A hedge fund
can seek the best price across many banks and electronic liquidity pools while

concentrating its credit, collateral and settlement relationship with one or a small
number of prime brokers. Before PB became widespread, a hedge fund's ability to
trade with a dealer depended much more directly on whether that dealer was
willing to establish a bilateral credit relationship with it, limiting the number of
counterparties it could access and often preventing it from trading on the same
electronic venues or at the same prices as large banks. PB substantially lowered this

Request for Quote (RFQ): It's a request
from a liquidity consumer to a liquidity
provider (like a bank) asking for the price at
which they are willing to buy or sell a
specific currency pair. The RFQ will
typically specify the currency pair, the
amount to be exchanged, and potentially
the desired settlement date. The FX
provider then responds with a quote, which
includes the exchange rate they are
offering.

### Page 21

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 21
barrier and helped non-bank institutions become much more active participants in
what had previously been a predominantly bank-to-bank market.
Prime brokerage also generates important operational and balance-sheet
efficiencies. Because trades with multiple executing dealers are ultimately
consolidated at the prime broker, clients can net exposures across counterparties,
reducing the amount of collateral and settlement infrastructure required. PB

arrangements also facilitate access to a wider range of liquidity providers—
including smaller and specialist banks—and support automated trade matching,
confirmation and straight-through processing. At the same time, the prime broker
assumes significant counterparty and operational risk: it is responsible to executing
dealers for trades conducted under its name and must therefore impose credit
limits and monitor the client's positions, leverage and trading activity in real time.

Prime brokerage thus does not eliminate credit risk; rather, it centralises it at the
prime broker while allowing execution to be dispersed across the wider market.
Today, a substantial share of spot FX trading is conducted through prime brokerage
arrangements (Figure 13).

4. Principal trading firms (non-bank dealers): Traditionally, banks acted as the
primary dealers, or liquidity providers, in the decentralised OTC FX market.
However, prime brokerage facilitated client access to primary FX trading venues
(EBS/Reuters). This allowed principal trading firms (PTFs), such as XTX Markets and
Citadel Securities, to leverage their technological capabilities for electronic market-

making, becoming key liquidity providers and serving a wide range of clients, from
regional banks to institutional investors.
While Principal Trading Firms (PTFs) are key FX market liquidity providers, they also
engage in proprietary trading (investing, hedging, speculating) including high-
frequency trading (HFT). HFT firms utilise sophisticated algorithms to execute a
large number of rapid trades, often holding positions for extremely short durations.

In this capacity, some PTF firms also act as liquidity consumers. This dual role has
contributed to the growth of algorithmic traders in FX, impacting inter-dealer
trading and the rise of other financial institutions' trading, as noted by BIS Triennial
Surveys (Figure 14).

5. Electronic communication networks (ECNs):  While prime brokerage has
facilitated client access to primary FX trading venues (EBS/Reuters ), numerous
secondary venues, such as Currenex and LMAX, have also emerged as a result of
competition driven by technological advances. This has enabled clients (non-
banks) to both provide and consume liquidity through limit orders or RFS/RFQ
model.
Beyond these venues, futures exchanges and dark pools are other alternative

liquidity sources. Exchanges utilise centralized limit order books with standardised
products, while dark pools operate with hidden order books, matching buy and sell
interest at a venue-calculated midpoint. BGC MidFX and FXSpotStream(FSS)
MidMatch are prominent examples of FX dark pools.
The rise of secondary and alternative trading venues has also changed how banks
manage inventory risk. Banks also utilise multiple venues for hedging client trades.

Crucially, the inter-dealer market is no longer exclusive to banks; some clients now
also provide liquidity through primary, secondary, and alternative venues.
1.5.2 Execution styles: voices versus electronic trading
This section explores the various FX execution styles, adhering to the Bank for
International Settlements (BIS) definitions for the dealer-client segment and the
inter-dealer segment.
Outer tier: Dealer-client segment
The two main styles of execution in the dealer-client (outer tier) segment are: Voice

and Electronic (BIS Triennial Survey). Each of these can further be classified as
direct or indirect. As per BIS, they are defined as follows:
1.  Voice-direct (telephone): Trades originated in person, by phone, by telefax
or through general messaging systems (eg Outlook, Gmail or Yahoo mail)
regardless of how trades are subsequently matched, and not
intermediated by a third party.

Inner tier: Inter-dealer segment
For trades that are not internalized (settled across own books), dealers can choose
between voice or electronic trading to manage remaining inventory. While
traditionally relying on primary venues like EBS/Reuters, they now also have the
option
of hedging through secondary or alternative venues. This expanded choice,
along with increased internalisation, contributes to the observed decline in inter-

dealer platform activity (Figure 17).
1.5.3 Trading Venues: Anonymity and Liquidity
Different trading venues can be distinguished based on their offerings in terms of
two key characteristics: pre-trade anonymity and liquidity firmness (Chaboud et al.,
2023). Pre-trade anonymity refers to the degree to which counterparties remain
unknown to each other before a trade is executed. Liquidity firmness, on the other
hand, describes the certainty of execution at a quoted price.

Pre-trade anonymity: The level of anonymity can range from fully disclosed to fully
anonymous. Multi-dealer platforms (MDPs), acting as aggregators of dealer
liquidity, typically operate on a fully disclosed basis. Clients, both financial
institutions and corporations, submit requests for quote (RFQs) via the platform to
multiple dealers, revealing their identity before trade execution. Similarly, bank-
owned single-dealer platforms also function with full disclosure, as the bank dealer

knows the identity of the client submitting the RFQ.
At the other end, primary venues running a centralized limit order book (such as
EBS/Reuters) generally operate on a fully anonymous basis. The displayed orders
do not show a source and all participants can be matched based on the typical price
and time priority rules of a CLOB. However, it is important to note that unlike
exchanges, this still preserves bilateral relationship of an OTC market structure

### Page 25

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 25
since only those that have established bilateral credit relationships, either directly
or via their PBs can participate on such venues. Exchange venues, on the other
hand, provide the highest possible level of pre-trade (and post-trade) anonymity,
basically by definition, as the exchange is the counterparty to each trade.
This anonymity stems from the exchange acting as the central counterparty to every
trade, effectively shielding the identities of the ultimate buyers and sellers. This

based on specific characteristics or trading styles. This allows participants to select
counterparties based on factors like execution quality, creating a tiered system of
interaction.
Liquidity firmness: The firmness of liquidity in FX trading, meaning the certainty of
execution at a quoted price, is significantly influenced by the practice of "last look."
This practice allows liquidity providers (LPs) a brief window, typically milliseconds,
to review a trade request before accepting or rejecting it (Oomen, 2016; Cartea,

Jaimungal, and Walton, 2019).
Last look was initially developed to address challenges posed by network latencies,
platform multiplicity, and varying technological sophistication among market
participants. Its acceptable use includes near-instantaneous checks for credit
availability and price validity. However, more controversial applications involve
imposing additional hold times before trade confirmation.
Liquidity on primary venues is generally considered firm, as last look is prohibited.

Conversely, last look is widely employed on secondary venues and universally
available on MDPs and SDPs, where LPs retain the option to use it. Consequently,
liquidity on these platforms is viewed as less firm, introducing uncertainty
regarding trade execution upon hitting a quoted price.
1.5.4 Current market structure challenges
The adoption of electronic trading has generally enhanced FX market quality.
Benefits include increased pre-trade transparency, improved operational efficiency

through electronic clearing and settlement, and tighter bid-ask spreads and lower
intraday volatility in the spot market.
Figure 18 and Figure 19, which track G10 FX high-frequency realized volatility and
top-of-the-book spreads respectively, both calculated using one-minute mid-price
changes, indicate a downward trend in these metrics. This suggests an
improvement in FX market quality over time. The sustained decrease in spreads
over time reflects intensified competition for client business in the FX market. The

proliferation of trading venues has fostered a competitive environment among
liquidity providers, driving them towards consistently narrower, approaching zero,
spreads.

Last look: This practice allows liquidity

milliseconds, to review a trade request
before accepting or rejecting it.
